`README.md`:

```md
<p align="center"><img src="docs/banner.svg" width="100%" alt="IDA Arsenal: Mark find and avoid in IDA, get the input back from angr."></p>

An IDA Pro 9.1 plugin that adds a symbolic path solver, input discovery, native crypto recognition and a few triage tools to the disassembler, with angr running in a separate Python process. It is for people doing crackme, CTF and malware-style reverse engineering who want to mark addresses in IDA and get an answer, without exporting the binary to a script every time.

## What it does

- **Path solver.** Right-click, Arsenal, Mark as find / Mark as avoid, and angr solves for the input that reaches one and not the other. Entry modes are `stdin`, `argv` and `call` (start at a function entry, with a return-value goal). Several named inputs can be solved together, with prefix, suffix, printable-only and per-byte constraints. Results show the input in hex and text and the blocks on the path.
- **Input map.** Finds stack variables that receive input (`read`, `fgets`, `scanf` and similar), names them `input1`, `input2`, and shows where each is compared. Names it assigned can be undone; names you set are left alone.
- **Triage scan.** Labels functions (input checking, debugger checks, network and so on) from decoded calls to recognized imports, never from names or strings alone. Each finding says what was and was not established.
- **Crypto recognition.** Finds custom RC4 in stripped or inlined code by operation shape and recovers key and data when they are constants. Reports evidence-backed candidates for AES, SHA-1/2, MD5, ChaCha/Salsa20, BLAKE2, Keccak and TEA/XTEA. See [docs/CRYPTO_ANALYSIS.md](docs/CRYPTO_ANALYSIS.md).
- **Z3 tab.** Solves SMT-LIB with a time budget, reports unsat cores, and classifies a Boolean expression as always true, always false, both feasible or unreachable (for opaque predicates).
- **Protections and unpacking.** Segment entropy, writable and executable mappings, packer section names, dispatcher-shaped control flow. UPX files can be unpacked to a new file after an integrity test.
- **Function dossier.** Exports a function as JSON plus a Frida hook, a GDB breakpoint script and an RVA/name table.
- **Go symbol recovery.** Parses `.gopclntab` (Go 1.18+) and renames `sub_` functions after a preview, with a rollback journal.
- **Debugger tab and listing tools.** A register, stack and memory view for IDA's debugger, and search/highlight overlays for the current function.

## Walkthrough

The tests include a six-byte checker. Build it, open it in IDA and press Ctrl+Alt+A to open the Arsenal dock.

```bash
cc -O0 -g -fPIE -pie tests/fixtures/check.c -o check-pie
```

Choose `stdin`, length 6, find `accepted`, avoid `rejected`, then Explore. The result is `IDASH!` (`494441534821`). To solve only the check, choose `call`, function `check`, length 6, return value 1.

The backend also runs without IDA: `venv/bin/python backend/solve.py request.json result.json` takes a JSON request (binary, mode, length, find and avoid addresses, timeout) and writes a result whose `status` is `found`, `not_found`, `incomplete`, `limit`, `timeout` or `error`. The exit code alone does not say whether an input was found. The request protocol is in [backend/README.md](backend/README.md).

## How it works

<p align="center"><img src="docs/readme-diagram.svg" width="100%" alt="Architecture diagram"></p>

<sub>Diagram source: [docs/diagrams/readme-diagram.mmd](docs/diagrams/readme-diagram.mmd)</sub>


IDA's embedded interpreter and angr's dependencies would conflict, so the plugin never imports angr. It starts `backend/solve.py` in a pinned virtualenv and exchanges JSON files in a per-job directory (mode 0700). A supervisor runs a worker in its own process group under an address-space cap (8 GiB by default) and kills it on timeout or Cancel. Before a solve, the plugin compares the input file's SHA-256 with the one in the database and refuses a database with patched bytes.

## Install

Tested with IDA Pro 9.1 (Python 3.14.7, PyQt5) on Linux x86-64. The solver runs in Python 3.12.13 with angr 10.0.4.post1 and z3-solver 5.1.0.0, pinned in `backend/requirements.lock`. The Go helper needs a Go toolchain; the test fixtures need a C compiler.

```bash
uv venv --python 3.12 venv
uv pip install --python venv/bin/python -r backend/requirements.lock
(cd backend && go build -o go-symbols go_symbols.go)      # only for Go symbol recovery
ln -s "$PWD/plugin/ida_arsenal.py" ~/.idapro/plugins/ida_arsenal.py
ln -s "$PWD/plugin/go_recover_arsenal.py" ~/.idapro/plugins/go_recover_arsenal.py
```

Restart IDA. FindCrypt-YARA, LazyIDA, Diaphora and Lighthouse were used alongside it but are not included or required. If the solver interpreter lives elsewhere, set `ARSENAL_PYTHON`. Jobs are written under `jobs/` and exports under `exports/` in this directory.

## Tests

`venv/bin/python -m pytest tests` does not need IDA and gives 360 passed and 19 skipped on the versions above; the optional `tests/fixtures/fetch_fauxware.sh` and `python3 tests/crypto_fixtures/build.py` enable the skipped ones (379 pass). The UI, Hex-Rays and debugger code was driven by scripted headless Qt sessions that depend on local databases and are not in this repository, so the tests do not cover it.

## Limitations

- Solver validation covers Linux x86-64 ELF only. PE, Mach-O and other architectures are not validated.
- It is symbolic execution from a file or function entry, not synchronized with a live debugger. A found input needs checking against the real program.
- Hard checkers can exceed the state budget or hit unsupported instructions (a modular-exponentiation loop ran out of states; an AVX-512 byte permute outside the modeled set stopped it). `limit` and `incomplete` are never reported as "no solution".
- Symbolic stdout goals are rejected; use address targets. Non-PIE binaries cannot be rebased.
- RC4 recognition found the cipher in 12 of 18 compiler/optimization builds in the author's matrix. Other crypto families are candidates only and do not imply key recovery.
- The process boundary caps memory. It is not a malware sandbox, and the backend never runs the target natively. Only the IDA Pro 9.1 and Python versions above have been tested.

## License

MIT, see [LICENSE](LICENSE). The test fixture `fauxware.c` is angr's and is downloaded rather than redistributed (BSD-2-Clause).

```

`backend/README.md`:

```md
# Arsenal symbolic execution service

Run with the dedicated Python environment, independent of IDAPython:

```
venv/bin/python backend/solve.py request.json result.json
```

The CLI always writes a structured result. Read `status`; exit status alone does
not mean a satisfying input was found. A worker process does all binary parsing
and symbolic execution. A supervisor kills it at timeout + 5 seconds, including
if a solver call stalls. Linux address space is capped at 8 GiB by default.
This isolates dependencies and bounds resources; it is not an OS security sandbox.
The backend never runs the input binary natively.
Tests cover Linux x86-64 ELF. Other CLE formats and architectures
are not validated here.

## Request protocol

For the default `engine="angr"`, required `binary` is a local ELF/PE/Mach-O path supported by CLE. Specify `mode`
(`stdin`, `argv`, or `call`), byte `length`, and a success condition: `find` addresses,
`stdout_find` text, or `return_value` for call mode. Multiple success conditions are
ORed. `avoid` addresses and `stdout_avoid` text take precedence. stdin is a fixed
length stream with EOF. argv inserts one symbolic argument between `argv_before`
and `argv_after`. `prefix` and `suffix` are UTF-8 text. `printable` defaults true;
disable it when inputs include newlines or binary data. argv bytes cannot be NUL.

`base_addr` is IDA's image base, including zero. All addresses are absolute within
that coordinate system; targets are passed as stop points so targets inside blocks
are respected. Without it CLE chooses its normal base.

Call mode requires `start_addr`. Default `call_args` is `[{"kind":"buffer"}]`:
a pointer to length symbolic bytes plus a terminating NUL. Buffer arguments all
refer to that same buffer. Integer arguments use `{"kind":"int","value":6}`.
The inferred prototype uses int for scalar arguments and return type. Supply a C
`prototype` string for other sizes/signatures; default architecture calling
convention is used. `return_bits` defaults 32. Additional `registers` is a map of
register name to integer. `memory` is a list of `{address, hex}` initial writes.
All numeric addresses accept integers or strings with a `0x` prefix.

`byte_constraints` is a list of `{offset, value}` or `{offset, min, max}`. Defaults:
`timeout=60`, `max_steps=10000`, `max_states=256`, `memory_mb=8192`. Exceeding a state
or step budget returns `limit`; states are never silently pruned to claim exhaustion.
Optional `solver_timeout_ms` bounds each solver query (1..86400000 ms); omitting
it retains the installed solver's default. Interrupted paths and direct query
interruptions return `incomplete`, never an exhausted-search verdict. Increase
both this query budget and `timeout` when a long SMT check needs more time.
For each byte, the backend folds printable, prefix/suffix, argv-NUL, and explicit
byte bounds into its symbolic domain. It represents a contiguous domain with the
minimum number of symbolic bits (for example printable ASCII uses seven bits per
byte), then zero-extends and offsets back to the original byte value. This applies
to all input modes and keeps the program-visible bytes unchanged.

Example direct function request (replace address and binary):

```json
{"binary":"/tmp/crackme","mode":"call","length":6,"start_addr":"0x1189",
 "base_addr":0,"prototype":"int check(const char *p)","return_value":1,
 "prefix":"IDA","timeout":30}
```

## Result and limitations

`status` is `found`, `not_found`, `incomplete`, `limit`, `timeout`, or `error`. Found results include
`input_hex`, `input_text`, `found_addr`, `trace` (basic block addresses), `stdout`,
`stats`, and `warnings`. The hex representation is authoritative for binary inputs.
Trace can contain external SimProcedure addresses and a synthetic return address.

Shared libraries use angr SimProcedures (`auto_load_libs=False`). Missing caller
context, system calls, unsupported instructions, uninitialized memory, external
state, and code using real runtime constructors can limit fidelity. Function mode
is a synthetic call, not a captured debugger process. Unknown values stay symbolic;
extra symbolic dependencies are reported. A found model needs concrete validation.
An exhausted search with errored or unconstrained paths reports `incomplete`.
Even `not_found` is scoped to the supplied model and initialized state.

Imported `strcmp` and `strncmp` calls use a bounded byte-accurate model when
the compared strings' lengths are proven (fixed-length symbolic argv or fully
concrete strings; `strncmp` can also use a concrete count). Comparisons are
bounded to 4096 bytes. Longer or unknown strings fall back to angr's standard
models; Arsenal never guesses a terminator for an unknown pointer. This covers
argv strings beyond angr's default symbolic-byte scan window while preserving
the fallback for unbounded pointers.

For Linux `MAP_ANONYMOUS`, the imported `mmap` wrapper ignores the descriptor
when the anonymous flag is concrete, matching Linux behavior even when the
caller passes fd 0. Mapped input files can be supplied with `files`, each
containing an absolute path and hex contents; files are bounded to 16 MiB each
and 64 MiB total. `fgets` records a unique destination and size bound for later
string models, and imported `strcspn` uses that proven bound (or a concrete
string length) to compute the exact symbolic result. Unbounded strings remain
an explicit modeling error instead of silently receiving an arbitrary result.
For Linux ELF imports with glibc dependency/version evidence, `srand`/`srandom`
and `rand`/`random` share glibc TYPE_3 generator state. The state preserves
branch-dependent seeds and draw counts when paths merge. `initstate`/`setstate`
raise explicit modeling errors because alternate generator types are not modeled.
The mmap shim is limited to known Linux ABIs using MAP_ANONYMOUS=0x20.
`fgets` corrects pointer/EOF/size return behavior around angr's bounded input
model; non-unique symbolic sizes are explicitly unsupported. For byte-addressable
files it consumes only through the first newline, preserves unread suffix bytes,
and keeps symbolic stream positions across calls. Packet streams and explicit
read-size concretization are rejected. `strcspn` revalidates remembered buffer
bounds after mutation. String comparison models stop once a mismatch or NUL
determines the result, avoiding needless reads beyond a terminated string.
Symbolic seed inversion can still be costly; a fully symbolic attempt against a
PRNG-seeded checker timed out in its final comparison.

## SMT-LIB / direct Z3 engine

Use the same supervised CLI with `engine="z3"`; a binary is not required:

```json
{"engine":"z3","timeout":60,"max_models":1,"smtlib":"(declare-const x (_ BitVec 8)) (assert (= (bvadd x #x01) #x00))"}
```

Results distinguish `sat`, `unsat`, and `unknown`, include one joint assignment
per model, and preserve `reason_unknown`. `other_model_values` reports arrays,
functions and other non-enumerated sorts, with explicit truncation. Contradictory
assertions return `unsat_core_assertions` (zero-based, sufficient but not necessarily
minimal). `max_models` is 1..16; only Boolean, integer and bit-vector variables
occurring in assertions participate in enumeration. SMT-LIB input is capped at
4 MiB. State-changing commands and solver option commands are rejected; timeout
and memory limits belong to the supervisor. This evaluates no Python source.
Overloaded free symbol names are rejected explicitly because the displayed
model is keyed by name; use distinct names for different sorts or arities.

Add `predicate` with a single Boolean SMT-LIB expression to classify a branch
under the script's assertions. The declarations/definitions in `smtlib` also
apply to this expression. A shared time budget covers the assumption check and
both branch checks. Classifications are `always_true`, `always_false`,
`both_feasible`, `unreachable` (contradictory assumptions), or `inconclusive`
(`status="unknown"`). `checks` includes each query status and witness models for
feasible branches. No native branch is modified or inferred automatically.
See `examples/z3/constant-predicate.json` and `overflow-counterexample.json`.
The proof pattern follows Z3's documented [incremental solver scopes](https://z3prover.github.io/papers/programmingz3.html).

For angr requests, `smt_export` names an output `.smt2` file. Arsenal snapshots
one active path approximately every 15 seconds between steps and again at the
end. It includes named aliases for the exact input byte vectors (big-endian hex
preserves input order). Partial snapshots do **not** assert a success goal and
do not include all active paths. A stalled query can leave an older snapshot.
Only existing Arsenal snapshots can be replaced; unrelated files are preserved.

The worker also installs exact Claripy models for three-source EVEX `PermI*`
VEX operations used by `VPERMI2*` and `VPERMT2*`, plus the 512-bit two-source
`Perm8x64` emitted for `VPERMB`. These operations have semantics and operand
orders that differ from angr's legacy AltiVec/PSHUFB handler. Chunked selectors
and a repeated-mask fast path keep symbolic wide-vector execution practical.
Models are limited to operations whose stock behavior is unsupported or
incorrect; the worker reports enabled models in its result warnings. For input
bytes constrained to one value or two adjacent values, the backend can encode
them as constants or one-bit choices directly, avoiding irrelevant symbolic
bits while preserving the same allowed byte set.

Stdout marker matching requires uniquely determined output. If output bytes or
their lengths remain symbolic, the backend returns an explicit error directing
you to find/avoid addresses. It never selects an arbitrary output model as a goal.

Pinned requirements are in `requirements.lock`. Current angr 10 embeds claripy:
`from angr import claripy`; old `import claripy` plugins need porting. Backend
research references: https://docs.angr.io/en/latest/core-concepts/states.html and
https://docs.angr.io/en/latest/advanced-topics/file_system.html .

Run reproducible tests:

```
venv/bin/python -m pytest tests/ -v
```

Cancellation: SIGTERM/SIGINT to the supervisor kills its solver process group and
writes `status=error`, `error="Cancelled by caller"`. Linux workers also arm a
parent-death SIGKILL, so a GUI hard kill cannot leave a running solver orphan.

Non-position-independent binaries manually rebased away from their linked image
base are rejected. CLE cannot repair absolute addresses in such binaries without
relocations. Use the original image base; rebasing PIE binaries is supported.

```

"""IDA Arsenal external symbolic execution backend."""

`backend/angr_compat.py`:

```py
"""Small, semantics-checked compatibility models for angr's VEX engine."""
from __future__ import annotations

import re


def _select_chunk(claripy, chunks, selector):
    """Select a power-of-two chunk with a balanced binary mux tree."""
    level = list(chunks)
    for bit in range(selector.size()):
        level = [claripy.If(selector[bit] == 1, level[index + 1], level[index])
                 for index in range(0, len(level), 2)]
    return level[0]


def install_avx512_permute_models():
    """Model VEX's three-operand EVEX Perm ops (VPERMI2*/VPERMT2*).

    angr deliberately rejects these operations because its generic AltiVec
    Perm implementation has different semantics. VEX provides the original
    destination, index vector, and second source as three operands. This
    model selects each lane from the concatenation of the two sources using
    the low log2(2*N) index bits.
    """
    import angr
    from angr.engines.vex.claripy import irop

    def expanded_mask_condition(value, lane_width):
        """Recognize VEX mask expansion without simplifying large ASTs."""
        if value.op != 'If':
            return None
        condition, when_true, when_false = value.args
        if when_true.op != 'BVV' or when_false.op != 'BVV':
            return None
        all_ones = (1 << lane_width) - 1
        true_value, false_value = when_true.args[0], when_false.args[0]
        if (true_value, false_value) == (all_ones, 0):
            return condition, True
        if (true_value, false_value) == (0, all_ones):
            return condition, False
        return None

    installed = []
    candidates = [name for name in irop.unsupported
                  if re.fullmatch(r'Iop_PermI(?:8|16|32|64)x(?:2|4|8|16|32|64)', name)]
    for name in candidates:
        attrs = irop.op_attrs(name)
        if attrs is None:
            continue

        # SimIROp rejects this family during construction because angr marks
        # its stock implementation unsafe. Build a normal dispatch object,
        # then replace its calculator with the exact three-source semantics.
        rejected = irop.UNSUPPORTED_EVEX_OPS
        irop.UNSUPPORTED_EVEX_OPS = frozenset(op for op in rejected if op != name)
        try:
            model = irop.SimIROp(name, **attrs)
        finally:
            irop.UNSUPPORTED_EVEX_OPS = rejected

        def calculate(args, *, op_name=name, vector_size=model._vector_size,
                      vector_count=model._vector_count):
            if len(args) != 3:
                raise angr.errors.UnsupportedIROpError(
                    f'{op_name} expected three source vectors, got {len(args)}')
            destination, indices, source = args
            selector_bits = (2 * vector_count - 1).bit_length()
            table = angr.claripy.Concat(source, destination)
            chunks = [table[(i + 1) * 64 - 1:i * 64]
                      for i in range(table.size() // 64)]
            lane_choices = [destination[(i + 1) * vector_size - 1:i * vector_size]
                            for i in range(vector_count)]
            lane_choices += [source[(i + 1) * vector_size - 1:i * vector_size]
                             for i in range(vector_count)]
            in_chunk_bits = (64 // vector_size).bit_length() - 1
            result = []
            for lane in range(vector_count):
                index_lane = indices[(lane + 1) * vector_size - 1:lane * vector_size]
                expanded = expanded_mask_condition(index_lane, vector_size)
                if expanded is not None:
                    condition, true_selects_last = expanded
                    result.append(angr.claripy.If(
                        condition, lane_choices[-1] if true_selects_last else lane_choices[0],
                        lane_choices[0] if true_selects_last else lane_choices[-1]))
                    continue
                selector = index_lane
                selector = selector[selector_bits - 1:0]
                if selector.op == 'BVV':
                    result.append(lane_choices[selector.args[0]])
                    continue
                chunk_selector = selector[selector_bits - 1:in_chunk_bits]
                offset = (selector[in_chunk_bits - 1:0]
                          if in_chunk_bits else angr.claripy.BVV(0, 1))
                if chunk_selector.op == 'BVV':
                    chunk = chunks[chunk_selector.args[0]]
                else:
                    chunk = _select_chunk(angr.claripy, chunks, chunk_selector)
                shift = angr.claripy.ZeroExt(64 - offset.size(), offset) * vector_size
                result.append(angr.claripy.LShR(chunk, shift)[vector_size - 1:0])
            return angr.claripy.Concat(*reversed(result))

        model._calculate = calculate
        irop.operations[name] = model
        irop.unsupported.discard(name)
        installed.append(name)

    # libVEX emits a distinct two-argument Perm8x64 for AVX-512 VPERMB. Its
    # argument order is (indices, source), unlike the older AltiVec/PSHUFB
    # Perm implementation (source, control), and VPERMB ignores selector bit
    # 7 instead of using it as a zeroing flag. Keep this exact model narrowly
    # scoped to the 512-bit IROp that the legacy handler mis-models.
    vpermb = irop.operations.get('Iop_Perm8x64')
    if vpermb is not None and vpermb._vector_size == 8 and vpermb._vector_count == 64:
        def calculate_vpermb(args):
            if len(args) != 2:
                raise angr.errors.UnsupportedIROpError(
                    f'Iop_Perm8x64 expected indices and source, got {len(args)} operands')
            indices, source = args
            lanes = [source[(i + 1) * 8 - 1:i * 8] for i in range(64)]
            chunks = [source[(i + 1) * 64 - 1:i * 64] for i in range(8)]
            result = []
            for lane in range(64):
                index_byte = indices[(lane + 1) * 8 - 1:lane * 8]
                expanded = expanded_mask_condition(index_byte, 8)
                if expanded is not None:
                    condition, true_selects_last = expanded
                    result.append(angr.claripy.If(
                        condition, lanes[63] if true_selects_last else lanes[0],
                        lanes[0] if true_selects_last else lanes[63]))
                    continue
                selector = index_byte[5:0]
                if selector.op == 'BVV':
                    result.append(lanes[selector.args[0]])
                    continue
                chunk_selector = selector[5:3]
                offset = selector[2:0]
                if chunk_selector.op == 'BVV':
                    chunk = chunks[chunk_selector.args[0]]
                else:
                    chunk = _select_chunk(angr.claripy, chunks, chunk_selector)
                shift = angr.claripy.ZeroExt(61, offset) * 8
                result.append(angr.claripy.LShR(chunk, shift)[7:0])
            return angr.claripy.Concat(*reversed(result))

        vpermb._calculate = calculate_vpermb
        installed.append('Iop_Perm8x64')
    return installed

```

`backend/go_symbols.go`:

```go
// Recover ELF Go function symbols from validated Go 1.18+ pclntab records.
// This tool parses the file; it never executes the input binary.
package main

import (
	"bytes"
	"debug/elf"
	"encoding/json"
	"fmt"
	"os"
	"runtime/debug"
	"syscall"
)

type Function struct {
	Name    string `json:"name"`
	Address uint64 `json:"address"`
	End     uint64 `json:"end"`
}
type Result struct {
	ImageBase uint64     `json:"image_base"`
	TextStart uint64     `json:"text_start"`
	Functions []Function `json:"functions"`
}

const maxPclnBytes = 128 * 1024 * 1024
const maxFunctions = 100000
const maxNamesBytes = 16 * 1024 * 1024

// resolveTextStart fails closed unless a header, symbol, or validated moduledata
// establishes runtime.text. The ELF .text section may contain a preceding CRT.
func resolveTextStart(f *elf.File, headerStart uint64, text *elf.Section, pclnAddr uint64, offsets []uint64, nfunc, firstPC, lastPC uint64, ptr uint64) (uint64, error) {
	start := headerStart
	if symtab := f.Section(".symtab"); symtab != nil {
		if symtab.Size > 32*1024*1024 {
			return 0, fmt.Errorf("symbol table exceeds size limit")
		}
		if uint64(symtab.Link) >= uint64(len(f.Sections)) ||
			f.Sections[symtab.Link].Size > 32*1024*1024 {
			return 0, fmt.Errorf("invalid or oversized symbol strings")
		}
		symbols, err := f.Symbols()
		if err != nil {
			return 0, fmt.Errorf("invalid ELF symbols: %w", err)
		}
		for _, symbol := range symbols {
			if symbol.Name == "runtime.text" {
				if start != 0 && start != symbol.Value {
					return 0, fmt.Errorf("runtime.text disagrees with pclntab header")
				}
				start = symbol.Value
				break
			}
		}
	}
	if start == 0 {
		// Go 1.27 removed pcHeader.textStart. Locate moduledata through the
		// pclntab pointer and validate every leading slice pointer/count.
		needle := make([]byte, ptr)
		if ptr == 8 {
			f.ByteOrder.PutUint64(needle, pclnAddr)
		} else {
			f.ByteOrder.PutUint32(needle, uint32(pclnAddr))
		}
		scanned := uint64(0)
		for _, section := range f.Sections {
			if section.Type != elf.SHT_PROGBITS || section.Flags&(elf.SHF_ALLOC|elf.SHF_WRITE) != elf.SHF_ALLOC|elf.SHF_WRITE {
				continue
			}
			if section.Flags&elf.SHF_COMPRESSED != 0 || section.Size > 128*1024*1024 || scanned+section.Size > 256*1024*1024 {
				return 0, fmt.Errorf("moduledata scan exceeds section limits")
			}
			scanned += section.Size
			raw, err := section.Data()
			if err != nil {
				return 0, err
			}
			for at := 0; at < len(raw); {
				found := bytes.Index(raw[at:], needle)
				if found < 0 {
					break
				}
				at += found
				candidateOffset := at
				at++
				if uint64(len(raw)-candidateOffset) < 24*ptr {
					continue
				}
				get := func(index uint64) uint64 {
					pos := uint64(candidateOffset) + index*ptr
					if ptr == 8 {
						return f.ByteOrder.Uint64(raw[pos : pos+8])
					}
					return uint64(f.ByteOrder.Uint32(raw[pos : pos+4]))
				}
				valid := true
				for index, expected := range map[uint64]uint64{1: offsets[0], 4: offsets[1], 7: offsets[2], 10: offsets[3], 13: offsets[4], 16: offsets[4]} {
					if pclnAddr > ^uint64(0)-expected || get(index) != pclnAddr+expected {
						valid = false
						break
					}
				}
				if !valid || get(17) != nfunc+1 || get(18) != nfunc+1 {
					continue
				}
				candidate := get(22)
				if candidate < text.Addr || candidate-text.Addr >= text.Size || lastPC > text.Size-(candidate-text.Addr) {
					continue
				}
				if get(20) != candidate+firstPC || get(21) != candidate+lastPC {
					continue
				}
				if start != 0 && start != candidate {
					return 0, fmt.Errorf("ambiguous moduledata text addresses")
				}
				start = candidate
			}
		}
	}
	if start < text.Addr || start-text.Addr >= text.Size {
		return 0, fmt.Errorf("cannot establish runtime.text from the header, ELF symbol, or validated moduledata")
	}
	return start, nil
}

func parseFunctions(f *elf.File, data []byte, text *elf.Section, pclnAddr uint64) ([]Function, uint64, error) {
	if len(data) < 8 || data[4] != 0 || data[5] != 0 ||
		(data[6] != 1 && data[6] != 2 && data[6] != 4) {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("invalid pclntab header")
	}
	order := f.ByteOrder
	magic := order.Uint32(data[:4])
	if magic != 0xfffffff0 && magic != 0xfffffff1 {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("only Go 1.18+ pclntab layouts are supported")
	}
	ptr := uint64(data[7])
	if (f.Class == elf.ELFCLASS64 && ptr != 8) ||
		(f.Class == elf.ELFCLASS32 && ptr != 4) {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("pclntab pointer size does not match ELF")
	}
	headerSize := uint64(8) + 8*ptr
	if ptr != 4 && ptr != 8 || uint64(len(data)) < headerSize {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("truncated pclntab header")
	}
	word := func(i uint64) uint64 {
		offset := 8 + i*ptr
		if ptr == 4 {
			return uint64(order.Uint32(data[offset : offset+4]))
		}
		return order.Uint64(data[offset : offset+8])
	}
	nfunc, nfile := word(0), word(1)
	if nfunc == 0 || nfunc > maxFunctions || nfile > 1000000 {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("pclntab counts exceed limits")
	}
	offsets := []uint64{word(3), word(4), word(5), word(6), word(7), uint64(len(data))}
	previous := headerSize
	for _, offset := range offsets {
		if offset < previous || offset > uint64(len(data)) {
			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("invalid pclntab subtable offsets")
		}
		previous = offset
	}
	funcData := data[offsets[4]:]
	tableSize := nfunc*8 + 4
	if tableSize > uint64(len(funcData)) {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("truncated pclntab function table")
	}
	firstPC := uint64(order.Uint32(funcData[:4]))
	lastPC := uint64(order.Uint32(funcData[nfunc*8 : nfunc*8+4]))
	start, err := resolveTextStart(f, word(2), text, pclnAddr, offsets, nfunc, firstPC, lastPC, ptr)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, 0, err
	}
	if text.Size > ^uint64(0)-text.Addr {
		return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("text address overflow")
	}
	textEnd := text.Addr + text.Size
	names := data[offsets[0]:offsets[1]]
	functions := make([]Function, 0, int(nfunc))
	nameBytes := 0
	for i := uint64(0); i < nfunc; i++ {
		entry := uint64(order.Uint32(funcData[i*8 : i*8+4]))
		end := uint64(order.Uint32(funcData[(i+1)*8 : (i+1)*8+4]))
		if entry >= end || start > textEnd || end > textEnd-start {
			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("invalid pclntab function address range")
		}
		info := uint64(order.Uint32(funcData[i*8+4 : i*8+8]))
		if info < tableSize || info > uint64(len(funcData)) || uint64(len(funcData))-info < 8 {
			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("invalid pclntab function record")
		}
		if uint64(order.Uint32(funcData[info:info+4])) != entry {
			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("pclntab entry and record disagree")
		}
		nameOffset := uint64(order.Uint32(funcData[info+4 : info+8]))
		if nameOffset >= uint64(len(names)) {
			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("invalid pclntab name offset")
		}
		limit := nameOffset + 4097
		if limit > uint64(len(names)) {
			limit = uint64(len(names))
		}
		length := bytes.IndexByte(names[nameOffset:limit], 0)
		if length <= 0 || length > 4096 {
			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("unterminated or oversized Go function name")
		}
		nameBytes += length
		if nameBytes > maxNamesBytes {
			return nil, 0, fmt.Errorf("Go names exceed total limit")
		}
		functions = append(functions, Function{string(names[nameOffset : nameOffset+uint64(length)]), start + entry, start + end})
	}
	return functions, start, nil
}

func recoverSymbols(path string) (*Result, error) {
	info, err := os.Stat(path)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	if !info.Mode().IsRegular() || info.Size() > 512*1024*1024 {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("input must be a regular ELF file no larger than 512 MiB")
	}
	f, err := elf.Open(path)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	defer f.Close()
	text, pcln := f.Section(".text"), f.Section(".gopclntab")
	if text == nil || pcln == nil {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("ELF requires .text and .gopclntab sections")
	}
	if pcln.Size > maxPclnBytes || pcln.Flags&elf.SHF_COMPRESSED != 0 {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("pclntab exceeds 128 MiB limit")
	}
	data, err := pcln.Data()
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	functions, textStart, err := parseFunctions(f, data, text, pcln.Addr)
	if err != nil {
		return nil, err
	}
	result := &Result{ImageBase: ^uint64(0), TextStart: textStart, Functions: functions}
	for _, p := range f.Progs {
		if p.Type == elf.PT_LOAD && p.Vaddr < result.ImageBase {
			result.ImageBase = p.Vaddr
		}
	}
	if result.ImageBase == ^uint64(0) {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("ELF has no load segment")
	}
	if len(result.Functions) == 0 {
		return nil, fmt.Errorf("no Go functions found")
	}
	return result, nil
}
func main() {
	debug.SetMemoryLimit(128 * 1024 * 1024)
	for resource, maximum := range map[int]uint64{syscall.RLIMIT_AS: 2 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024, syscall.RLIMIT_DATA: 512 * 1024 * 1024} {
		if err := syscall.Setrlimit(resource, &syscall.Rlimit{Cur: maximum, Max: maximum}); err != nil {
			fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, "cannot set parser resource limits:", err)
			os.Exit(1)
		}
	}
	if len(os.Args) != 2 {
		fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, "usage: go-symbols ELF")
		os.Exit(2)
	}
	result, err := recoverSymbols(os.Args[1])
	if err != nil {
		fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}
	if err := json.NewEncoder(os.Stdout).Encode(result); err != nil {
		fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, err)
		os.Exit(1)
	}
}

```

`backend/libc_models.py`:

```py
"""ABI-qualified libc helpers and merge-aware glibc random state."""
import angr
from cle.backends.elf.elf import ELF

try:
    from angr import claripy
except ImportError:
    import claripy


def linux_anonymous_mmap_supported(project):
    """Only these Linux ABIs use the modeled MAP_ANONYMOUS value 0x20."""
    return (isinstance(project.loader.main_object, ELF)
            and project.simos.name == 'Linux'
            and project.arch.name in {'AMD64', 'X86', 'ARMEL', 'ARMHF', 'AARCH64'})


def glibc_random_import(project, relocation):
    """Require a Linux ELF dependency and the import's glibc symbol version."""
    obj = project.loader.main_object
    symbol = getattr(relocation, 'symbol', None)
    return (isinstance(obj, ELF) and project.simos.name == 'Linux'
            and 'libc.so.6' in obj.deps
            and str(getattr(symbol, 'version', '')).startswith('GLIBC_'))


def seed_words(seed):
    """Initialize glibc TYPE_3 using signed Park-Miller steps and 310 warmups."""
    seed = seed[31:0] if seed.size() > 32 else claripy.ZeroExt(32 - seed.size(), seed)
    seed = claripy.If(seed == 0, claripy.BVV(1, 32), seed)
    words = [seed]
    for _ in range(1, 31):
        previous = words[-1]
        hi = claripy.SDiv(previous, claripy.BVV(127773, 32))
        lo = claripy.SMod(previous, claripy.BVV(127773, 32))
        value = claripy.BVV(16807, 32) * lo - claripy.BVV(2836, 32) * hi
        words.append(claripy.If(claripy.SLT(value, claripy.BVV(0, 32)),
                                value + claripy.BVV(2147483647, 32), value))
    for _ in range(310):
        words, _ = step_words(words)
    return tuple(words)


def step_words(words):
    """Normalize the ring after each draw so front/rear offsets stay at 3/0."""
    updated = list(words)
    value = updated[3] + updated[0]
    updated[3] = value
    return tuple(updated[1:] + updated[:1]), claripy.LShR(value, 1)


class GlibcRandomState(angr.SimStatePlugin):
    """State that preserves branch-dependent seeds and draw counts on merge."""

    def __init__(self, words=None):
        super().__init__()
        self.words = tuple(words) if words is not None else seed_words(claripy.BVV(1, 32))

    @angr.SimStatePlugin.memo
    def copy(self, memo):
        return GlibcRandomState(self.words)

    def merge(self, others, merge_conditions, common_ancestor=None):
        if merge_conditions is None:
            raise angr.errors.SimMergeError('glibc random state requires explicit merge conditions')
        self.words = tuple(claripy.ite_cases(
            [(condition, other.words[index])
             for condition, other in zip(merge_conditions[1:], others)], value)
            for index, value in enumerate(self.words))
        return True

    def widen(self, others):
        raise angr.errors.SimMergeError('Widening glibc random state is unsupported')

    def seed(self, value):
        self.words = seed_words(value)

    def draw(self):
        self.words, value = step_words(self.words)
        return value

```
